2026-05-20: created tessellated-solid-facet-orientation incident — `G4TessellatedSolid::SetSolidClosed()` issues `GeomSolids1001: negative cubic volume` on TSdAHelicalTube; 197-of-200 helical050a workers affected; cascades into ~28M `GeomNav1002` stuck-track events per iteration. Preflight surface-check doesn't catch internal facet winding. Root cause: facet vertex order in `makeHelicalPlug()` (constructTSdA.cc:58-128). Likely explains helical050a's 40× calo bump vs legacy helical050.
2026-05-20: added `scan_logs` end-of-workflow node to graph-runner (between harvest and evaluate). Walks worker .log derived from each `<stage>_outputs.txt`, xargs-parallel greps for G4Exception/Stuck Track/GeomSolids1001/GeomNav1002/Error/Warning/FATAL/SEGV, writes `<grid_root>/<config>/scan_logs/report.tsv` + JSON, populates state.scan_report. Report-only — no leaderboard mutation, no iteration gating. In-flight chains unaffected (old topology compiled in RAM).
2026-05-20: corrected tessellated-solid-facet-orientation root cause — static analysis of all 8 side triangles + 2 end caps in `makeHelicalPlug` shows facet winding is **correct** (front -ŷ, right +x̂, back +ŷ, left -x̂, bottom -ẑ, top +ẑ at angle=0). Real cause is **self-intersection at extreme aspect ratios**: helical050a's `dy/dx=1044` (essentially a 2D ribbon) + 4.6°/slice twist makes corner arc per slice (8.8 mm) 2.3× the axial step (3.89 mm) → consecutive slices' side facets cross → G4 divergence-theorem volume double-counts overlap with reversed sign → net negative. Predicate: `ratio = dy·angle_rad/(2·halflength) ≳ 1` flags degeneracy. Fix options revised in incident page: (a) source-side throw on negative `GetCubicVolume`, (b) Python pre-render reject in HelicalMode (no rebuild), (c) both.
2026-05-20: resolved tessellated-solid-facet-orientation — replaced facet construction in `makeHelicalPlug` with stacked analytic G4TwistedBox: N=ceil(|angle|/85°) segments unioned via G4UnionSolid; segment 0 wrapped in G4DisplacedSolid so the assembly origin sits at the geometric midpoint. Per-segment z-rotation `phi_i = -angle/2 + (i+0.5)·angle/N` matches consecutive faces (continuous helix). 85° cap stays under G4VTwistedFaceted's hard π/2 limit. Verified on helical050a (angle=459.591°, N=6): zero GeomSolids errors, GDML shows 6 twistedbox + 0 tessellated, mu2e -n 1 exit 0. Empirical: nsteps=500 alone does NOT fix it (topological self-intersection, not tessellation density); naive single G4TwistedBox throws GeomSolids0002 above 90° twist. Grid tarball repackage (task #53) still pending.
2026-05-20: updated muse-backing-pattern — incremental rebuild gotcha: `muse build Offline` reports "up to date" without relinking changed lib; target the .so explicitly (`muse build build/al9-prof-e29-p094/Offline/lib/libmu2e_Mu2eG4.so`). Object-file targets in package subdirs fail with "Do not know how to make File target" because scons names objects under `build/.../tmp/Mu2eG4/src/foo.os`.
2026-05-20: updated preflight — major caveat: `cmd_preflight` does NOT load the patched `libmu2e_Mu2eG4.so` (only sources stock CVMFS Run1Bak setup), so helical-plug-specific bugs are invisible to preflight. Helical plug constructed via stock Offline code, not the shipped patch. To fix, source local muse setup or extracted Code/setup.sh before mu2e -n 1.
2026-05-20: corrected tessellated-solid-facet-orientation mechanism (second pass) — earlier "consecutive slices' side facets cross" narrative was wrong. Real mechanism is **per-slice broad-face warp**: G4 splits each non-planar broad face (±x) into two triangles along a fixed diagonal; warp height `≈ dy·(angle/N)/4` (helical050a: 7.12 mm per slice) far exceeds plate thickness (`2·dx = 0.21 mm`), so the +x triangle pair crosses through the −x triangle pair *within the same slice*. Crossover predicate `N_crit ≈ dy·angle_rad/(8·dx) ≈ 3390` for helical050a. nsteps=500 fails because 500 << 3390; nsteps≥3400 would actually work (no source patch needed) but at ~50× facet count the stacked-twistedbox fix is preferred. Integrated volume inflation: 32.1 vs ~18 cm³ → ~80% mass error in every pre-fix grid job → bo-helical leaderboard biased; pre-fix rows should be re-run.
2026-05-20: produced side-by-side helical050a GDML evidence in `gdml_dumps/`: `mu2e_helical050a_tessellated.gdml` (old broken lib, LD_PRELOAD'd, 1 tessellated solid w/ 4002 facets, GeomSolids1001 thrown live) vs `mu2e_helical050a_twistedbox.gdml` (new patched lib, 6 twistedbox + 5 union intermediates, zero warnings). Pattern: LD_PRELOAD against Run1Bak works when the tarball Code/setup.sh's `muse setup` would conflict with an already-set Musing.

2026-05-22: Empirical root cause for "Minimal physics list breaks mubeam" via SimParticleAnalyzer on unfiltered g4run (100 events, same input/geom). Baseline: 8/108 mu- end in Target_Al Z-window, all stoppingCode=32 muMinusCaptureAtRest, KE=0. Minimal: 0/108 in target Z-window, 54/108 fly straight to world boundary at z=52,265 mm with stoppingCode=182 CoupledTransportation and KE unchanged from baseline (20 in [1,10] MeV, 34 in [10,100] MeV). Confirms option (a) — muons fly through Al because Minimal lacks muIoni, not (b) wrong end-process or (c) stop-in-wrong-volume. Diagnostic kept in /tmp/g4_mubeam_bench/{diag_simp_*.fcl, analyze_diag.py}. — g4-speed-knobs

2026-05-22: Enumerated accepted physicsListName strings in Offline/Mu2eG4/src/physicsListDecider.cc (v10_07_00). 3 hard-coded Mu2e names at lines 69-81 (Minimal, MinDEDX, ErrorPhysicsList); everything else delegated to G4PhysListFactory at line 89 — accepts Shielding[M], FTFP_BERT, QGSP_BERT, QBBC, and _EMV/_EMX/_EMY/_EMZ variants with zero code change. MinDEDX is an EM-only ablation arm: muIoni present, no G4StoppingPhysics → muons slow to rest but don't capture. 2026-05-22: 5-way physics-list bench on mubeam (300 events, helical011 geom). Big wins: FTFP_BERT and FTFP_BERT_EMV both give ~−48% wall vs ShieldingM with TargetStops/PolyStops within 1σ Poisson — ~2× speedup, zero code change. MinDEDX is the fastest viable arm at −64% wall (TargetStops 34 vs baseline 33, PolyStops 8 vs 7) — refutes earlier prediction it would give 0 stops; root cause is TargetStopFilter selects on kinematic state (KE≈0 in Al), not end-process code, so muIoni-only lists pass the filter even without G4StoppingPhysics. QBBC crashes rc=1 with "process not in ProcessCode enum" — Mu2e Offline/MCDataProducts/inc/ProcessCode.hh is a hidden runtime constraint on physics-list substitution; needs enum extension + rebuild. Recommend FTFP_BERT as zero-code grid A/B arm; MinDEDX needs SimParticle endpoint validation before production. — g4-speed-knobs
2026-05-22: MinDEDX kinematic validation via SimParticleAnalyzer 3-way diff (baseline/Minimal/MinDEDX, 100 events). MinDEDX puts 9 mu- at KE≈0 in Target_Al Z window vs baseline 8 (Δ<1σ); end-Z histogram bin-by-bin match (87/3/5/0/9/0/4 vs 88/1/6/0/8/0/5); end-KE distribution bit-identical globally and in target window. Total SimParticles 35,827→1,315 (27× fewer secondaries — origin of −64% wall). One caveat: MinDEDX fires code_31 at-rest process where baseline fires code_32 muMinusCaptureAtRest — kinematic outcome identical but stoppingCode metadata differs; production-blocker if any downstream stage filters on stoppingCode==32. — g4-speed-knobs
2026-05-22: Added GFlash/G4FastSimulationManagerProcess scope note + production-blocker checklist + stacking-action orthogonality to g4-speed-knobs.md Open questions. 2026-05-22: MinDEDX production-blocker CONFIRMED via Explore grep. `Offline/Mu2eUtilities/src/simParticleList.cc:15` (`stoppedMuMinusList`) hard-equals `ProcessCode::muMinusCaptureAtRest` (code 32). Consumers: `CeEndpoint_module.cc:120`, `FlatMuonDaughterGenerator_module`, `Pileup_module` — all reached via `mustops_ce` stage. MinDEDX code-31 stops dropped silently → CeEndpoint throws. 2026-05-22: 4-arm stacking bench (FTFP_BERT × minRangeCut, 300 events parallel) — minRangeCut does NOT stack on FTFP_BERT. ShieldingM+rc=0.05 was −6%; FTFP_BERT+rc=0.05 is −1.8% and FTFP_BERT+rc=0.10 is 0% — both within run-to-run jitter. Inference: ShieldingM's cuttable bottleneck was the heavy EM cascade work; FTFP_BERT already has a lighter EM + faster hadronic so minRangeCut has nothing left to suppress. Use FTFP_BERT alone for the grid A/B; second knob buys nothing. Stop counts in all 3 FTFP arms within Poisson noise of baseline. — g4-speed-knobs
2026-05-22: 3k-event 5-way bench with aggressive minRangeCut sweep (0.5/1.0/5.0 mm = 50×/100×/500× default). Confirms 300-event "no stacking on FTFP_BERT" finding wasn't statistics-limited: wall delta is ±4% with no monotone trend across rc values. Surprising: TargetStops shifts only 1.2σ even at rc=5.0 mm — muon trajectory is not affected, only EM secondary cascade depth. Important corollary: CE-harness (g4test_03) rc=1.0 → −18.5% does NOT transfer to mubeam (mubeam shows ~0%) — CE-harness extrapolation overstates minRangeCut's value at the production stage. — g4-speed-knobs
2026-05-22: Enumerated G4 ODE stepper choices in Mu2eWorld.cc:440-477 (10 registered names, throws "Unrecognized stepper" at :479). Default is G4DormandPrince745 set at prolog.fcl:47. Hidden constraint at physicsListDecider.cc:152-157: decayMuonsWithSpin: true requires *WSpin steppers (G4ClassicalRK4WSpin or G4DormandPrince745WSpin). Our default chain (MuBeamResampler + epilog_1b) does NOT set decayMuonsWithSpin so all 10 steppers are legal. Helix-aware variants (G4HelixSimpleRunge, G4HelixImplicitEuler) designed for solenoidal fields are the strongest candidates to beat the DormandPrince default. — g4-speed-knobs
2026-05-22: 5-arm stepper bench (FTFP_BERT × {dp745, helixSR, helixIE, rk4, bs23}, 3k events). Default G4DormandPrince745 wins clean — no swap helps. Surprise #1: helix-aware steppers (HelixSR +7%, HelixIE +5%) LOSE despite TS being solenoidal — they assume pure-helix motion, degrade in non-helical regions (DS/calo/world) that mubeam transport spans; dp745's adaptive step expansion in low-gradient regions wins on net. Surprise #2: G4BogackiShampine23 is 2× slower because at the tight epsilonMin/Max=1e-5 (prolog.fcl:53-54) the lower-order 3(2) method needs dramatically more steps to hit the same error bound — lower-order adaptive RK is an anti-pattern at this tolerance. Subtle caveat: all 4 non-default steppers gave higher PolyStops (88-95) than dp745 (80) closer to baseline ShieldingM's 96 — joint prob ~6% by chance, may indicate dp745+FTFP has small systematic PolyStop suppression; TargetStops unaffected. — g4-speed-knobs

Always grep the tar's mu2e.fcl for the edited knob before declaring a chain ran the intended variant. Per-stage idempotence is a sharp tool here: stale tar will be re-used unless explicitly deleted. — jobsub-disk-quota-stderr-swallowed

2026-05-24: bo-helical — PC01 first-round assessed (harvested ~40 min, much faster than ~8h plan estimate, likely empty queue). Soft signals: realized MPD 0.121 (vs baseline median ~0.075), dx span [0.094,4.835] full box (vs ~2.0), best obj=2.589 (highest of any round). But feasible-frac=1/8 tied with baseline; one feasible point lands at sob=0.718 (lower than baseline sob~1.29). Pred-vs-realized calo calibration is the next gap. Verdict: NEED 2ND ROUND despite strict-rule "REVERT" flag — the gate condition (feasible<3) is hit by baseline too, so it doesn't indict the pessimistic prior. ΔHV_ref metric is degenerate at calo<2e-6 cap: per-round HV gain identically 0.0 for every round (historical PF dominates new feasible points). Plans proposing ΔHV_ref as decision gate cannot discriminate in this regime — use soft signals or loosen the cap (e.g. 5e-6) — bo-helical

2026-06-04: scalarized-objective — added alpha-FREE reporting alternative: best-sob-at-calo-budget over all 343 foils evals (v1+v2+v3); clean/knee/max = sob@calo<={1e-6,1e-5,inf}={0.74,3.05,3.93}; trade-off steep-then-flat (~80% of max sob reached by calo<=1e-5 = sweet spot); 1e-6 point sparse (n=5, at calo floor) and extreme; picking calo budget B = interpretable version of pinning alpha — scalarized-objective
2026-06-04: scalarized-objective — the "knee" at calo~1e-5 is SOFT not a sharp inflection: empirical Pareto front = 44 non-dominated pts (346 evals); marginal slope dS/sqrtB per 1e-6 calo drops only ~3x across 1e-5 (~0.26 below -> ~0.083 above), per-segment slopes noisy 0.01-1.7; prefer stating "~80% of max signal by calo<=1e-5" over asserting a sharp knee; provisional (front shifts with new evals) — scalarized-objective

2026-06-06: closed-loop-bo-design — added two facts from arch-review grilling of `_child_terminal_via_checkpoint`: (1) `compile(checkpointer=saver)` re-wires the checkpointer per call, must compile inner graph ONCE outside barrier `while True:` loop (per-tick recompile would be ~180 wasted compiles/round at q=5, POLL=300s, 3h round); current pattern at closed_loop.py:486-494 is load-bearing. (2) `MemorySaver` has no snapshot-write API — unit-testing the terminal branch (snap.values + step>=1) requires running >=1 super-step through a compiled graph; can't inject fake snapshots. Fresh-thread branch is testable with `from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver` + `get_state` on a never-run thread — closed-loop-bo-design

2026-06-06: closed-loop-bo-design — two-critic refactor review surfaced architectural facts: (1) barrier loop at graph/closed_loop.py:459-475 calls `_child_in_leaderboard` + `_child_is_broken` + `is_child_terminal` per child per tick — first re-imports autoresearch_bo_michael, re-flocks + reparses the leaderboard TSV via load_history(); same triple also appears at `_already_running` (closed_loop.py:368-372) and `assign_names` (:344). Single `child_status()` query is a pure-refactor leverage point — three call sites, priority order today is implicit in `or` short-circuit. (2) `_scan_one_stage` (pipeline_io.py:312-339) spawns 9 subprocess fan-outs per stage × 4 stages = 36 grep subprocesses per harvest; single `grep -EHc '(p1|p2|…)'` over file list would cut ~9× + drop brittle `rpartition(":")` parser. (3) `run_preflight` vs `run_evaluate` (pipeline_io.py:98,405) are NOT collapsible: timeouts diverge (PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_S vs hardcoded 120), failure semantics diverge (TimeoutExpired→string vs raise), return shapes diverge ((status,tail) vs (float|None,tail)); a unified `run_driver_verb` becomes the strategy object we already have, renamed — closed-loop-bo-design
2026-06-06: closed-loop-bo-design — two-critic review killed proposed `child_status()` unification of `_child_in_leaderboard` + `_child_is_broken` + `is_child_terminal`. Three call sites are three different questions, not one: `node_assign_names` (closed_loop.py:344) is name-collision skip with no child_graph yet; `_already_running` (:368-372) is a *liveness/idempotency* guard against double-submit driven by `state_dir.glob("*_cluster.txt")` (a cluster.txt+no-leaderboard child is RUNNING not done — category error to share enum); barrier (:459-475) is done-from-my-POV. `barrier-false-positive-round1.md` documents 4 distinct regressions where mis-ordering the terminal check declared false convergence — load-bearing precision, not stylistic. Real win the unification masked: `_child_in_leaderboard` calls `bo.MODES[mode].load_history()` (autoresearch_bo_michael.py:164, full TSV csv.DictReader reparse) per child per tick — q×ticks reparses where 1 would do. Cheaper fix: hoist one `load_history()` per tick into `{p.cfg for p in ...}` set in `node_barrier`, replace per-name `_child_in_leaderboard` with set membership; ~5 lines, ~q× fewer reparses, zero semantic shift — closed-loop-bo-design

Measured: load_history() on 118-row TSV runs in ~0.4 ms; at q=10 that's 4 ms saved per CLOSED_LOOP_BARRIER_POLL_SEC=300s tick (0.001% of poll period); ~1-2 s saved per round at q=2-3, 60s poll, 6-12h rounds. Genuinely wiki-worthy hazard surfaced independently: `load_history` (autoresearch_bo_michael.py:164-174) does NOT acquire `_flock_ex` — only `append_history` does (line 177). 2026-06-06: closed-loop-bo-design — lockless-read hazard fixed: added `_flock_sh` shared-lock helper (autoresearch_bo_michael.py:76-92) and applied to `load_history` (:181) AND `load_pending` (:303). load_pending had the same asymmetry as load_history — lockless reads against `append_pending`/`remove_pending` which both take `_flock_ex` (lines 300, 314); the r-w-t cycle in remove_pending made torn-read worse than leaderboard append. SH/EX pair: writers block readers, readers block only writers — concurrent q-child reads don't contend with each other. All 70 tests pass — closed-loop-bo-design

2026-06-06: production-target-stickman — corrected coupling buckets: DPA and thermal have DIFFERENT geometry dependence. rOut is dominantly a THERMAL knob (radiating surface), NOT a DPA knob (local fluence is rOut-independent as long as rOut ≥ few·σ). 2026-06-06: production-target-stickman — CRITICAL: plate-to-plate pitch is plateLugThickness, NOT spacerHalfLength (constructTargetPS.cc:1659-1716 z-march: _currentZ += plateLugThickness(i)); inter-plate gap = plateLugThickness − plateThickness (defaults 6−5=1mm); spacer affects only single end-ring↔first-plate gap, dead knob for production physics. Envelope identity: 2·halfStickmanLength = 2·supportRingLength + 4·spacerHalfLength + Σ plateLugThickness (=232.2mm at defaults). plateLugThickness<plateThickness is silent overlap — defensive check belongs in forker — production-target-stickman

2026-06-07: bo-prodtarget — selected v1 thermal proxy = max_i (Edep_i / mass_i) [Gy/POT] (peak specific dose), NOT raw Edep. Rationale: peak T scales monotonically with volumetric power density = ρ·(Edep/mass); raw Edep can't distinguish two plates with same heat in different mass. Edep/mass scales as 1/rOut² at fixed beam profile (explains why rOut is dominant thermal knob despite being DPA-neutral). Mass computed by forker from geom knobs (no G4 round-trip). Bi-objective qNEHVI: maximize (muons_per_POT, −peak_dose_per_POT). Enable via SDConfig.enableSD: [virtualdetector, ProductionTargetCoreSection, ProductionTargetFinSection] in POT.fcl — no source patch — bo-prodtarget

2026-06-07: skopt gotcha — `opt.tell(x, y)` decrements `n_initial_points` budget INCLUDING the penalty tells `ask_buildable` makes during constraint-rejection retries (autoresearch_bo_michael.py:268-279). Means bumping n_initial_points=q as a cold-start fix is fragile: q=2 + any rejection burns the Sobol budget and same RuntimeError re-fires. 2026-06-07: cold-start fix decided (3-agent grilling) — Option 3 precheck `len(opt.Xi)==0 -> opt.space.rvs(q, rs=42^len(opt.Xi))` chosen over n_initial_points bump (penalty-tell drain) and hand-tell seed (y-scale mismatch breaks GP kernel amplitude — prodtarget obj ~1e-4 vs other modes ~1). Skopt error fires ONCE per Optimizer lifetime (`_n_initial_points<=0 AND models==[]`); auto-recovers after first real tell. skopt.Space.rvs works pre-fit. Precheck > try/except (no version-fragile string match, no shadowing real GP failures). prodtarget rejection-rate ~91% per-draw → P(all 20 retries fail) ≈ 15% (acceptable; gold-plate later if it bites) — prodtarget-propose-skopt-empty-init

2026-06-28: foilsflash null MECHANISM (hypothesis) — dE/dx-range vs X0 asymmetry: extra foils are a MUON range-degrader (slow ~few-MeV muon, range ~mm → µm-mm foil shifts stop distribution → strong sob knob) but flash electrons are relativistic/MIP (sub-mm-1mm low-Z foil ~0.001-0.01 X0 → negligible → weak flash knob). 2026-06-28: foilsflash null CORRECTED (operator pushback, data-checked) — it IS a weak electron knob, NOT inert (electrons multiple-scatter + shower secondaries off thin foils → tracker; 4.1% flash CoV above noise is that real effect). Retracted BOTH prior mechanism guesses ('graze on-axis', 'muon-knob-not-electron-knob/thin→negligible'). Leaderboard n=15: corr(material,flash)=+0.08 (flash NOT bulk-material-driven), corr(material,sob)=-0.59 (more material lowers sob, wants thin), corr(sob,flash)=+0.26. So foils move flash WEAKLY (~14% vs 111% sob range) via which-foil/scatter-geometry not grams. Why weak needs sim-level radial/energy dist of EleBeamFlash hits, not leaderboard. LESSON: stop asserting unverified mechanisms; check data first — bo-foilsflash
